\xi_{1}+\xi_{2}+\xi_{3}=\xi\geq 0
we calculate

\begin{split}p_{++-}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3})&=\frac{|\xi|^{3/2}\big(|\xi_{1}|^{1/2}|\xi_{3}|^{1/2}+|\xi_{2}|^{1/2}|\xi_{3}|^{1/2}-|\xi_{1}|^{1/2}|\xi_{2}|^{1/2}\big)}{8i}\qquad\text{ if }\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3}>0,\\
p_{++-}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3})&=\frac{|\xi|^{3/2}\big(|\xi|^{1/2}|\xi_{3}|^{1/2}-|\xi_{2}|^{1/2}|\xi|^{1/2}-|\xi_{2}|^{1/2}|\xi_{3}|^{1/2}\big)}{8i}\qquad\text{ if }\xi_{1}>0,\,\xi_{2},\xi_{3}<0,\\
p_{++-}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3})&=\frac{|\xi|^{3/2}\big(|\xi|^{1/2}|\xi_{3}|^{1/2}-|\xi_{1}|^{1/2}|\xi|^{1/2}-|\xi_{1}|^{1/2}|\xi_{3}|^{1/2}\big)}{8i}\qquad\text{ if }\xi_{2}>0,\,\xi_{1},\xi_{3}<0,\\
p_{++-}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3})&=\frac{|\xi|^{3/2}\big(|\xi_{1}|^{1/2}|\xi_{2}|^{1/2}-|\xi_{1}|^{1/2}|\xi|^{1/2}-|\xi_{2}|^{1/2}|\xi|^{1/2}\big)}{8i}\qquad\text{ if }\xi_{3}>0,\,\xi_{1},\xi_{2}<0,\end{split}

and

(7.63)

\begin{split}p_{++-}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3})&=\frac{(a^{1}_{3}-a^{2}_{3})(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3})}{-8i}\qquad\text{ if }\xi_{1},\xi_{2}>0,\,\xi_{3}<0,\\
p_{++-}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3})&=\frac{(a^{1}_{3}-a^{2}_{3})(\xi_{3},\xi_{1},\xi_{2})}{8i}\qquad\text{ if }\xi_{1},\xi_{3}>0,\,\xi_{2}<0,\\
p_{++-}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3})&=\frac{(a^{1}_{3}-a^{2}_{3})(\xi_{3},\xi_{2},\xi_{1})}{8i}\qquad\text{ if }\xi_{2},\xi_{3}>0,\,\xi_{1}<0.\end{split}

Therefore, after algebraic simplifications,

(7.64)

\begin{split}\frac{ip_{++-}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3})}{|\xi|^{1/2}-|\xi_{1}|^{1/2}-|\xi_{2}|^{1/2}+|\xi_{3}|^{1/2}}&=\frac{|\xi|^{3/2}\big(|\xi|^{1/2}+|\xi_{1}|^{1/2}+|\xi_{2}|^{1/2}-|\xi_{3}|^{1/2}\big)}{16}\,\,\,\text{ if }\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3}>0,\\
\frac{ip_{++-}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3})}{|\xi|^{1/2}-|\xi_{1}|^{1/2}-|\xi_{2}|^{1/2}+|\xi_{3}|^{1/2}}&=\frac{|\xi|^{3/2}\big(|\xi|^{1/2}+|\xi_{1}|^{1/2}-|\xi_{2}|^{1/2}+|\xi_{3}|^{1/2}\big)}{16}\,\,\,\text{ if }\xi_{1}>0,\,\xi_{2},\xi_{3}<0,\\
\frac{ip_{++-}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3})}{|\xi|^{1/2}-|\xi_{1}|^{1/2}-|\xi_{2}|^{1/2}+|\xi_{3}|^{1/2}}&=\frac{|\xi|^{3/2}(|\xi|^{1/2}-|\xi_{1}|^{1/2}+|\xi_{2}|^{1/2}+|\xi_{3}|^{1/2})}{16}\,\,\,\text{ if }\xi_{2}>0,\,\xi_{1},\xi_{3}<0,\\
\frac{ip_{++-}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3})}{|\xi|^{1/2}-|\xi_{1}|^{1/2}-|\xi_{2}|^{1/2}+|\xi_{3}|^{1/2}}&=\frac{|\xi|^{3/2}\big(|\xi|^{1/2}-|\xi_{1}|^{1/2}-|\xi_{2}|^{1/2}-|\xi_{3}|^{1/2}\big)}{16}\,\,\,\text{ if }\xi_{3}>0,\,\xi_{1},\xi_{2}<0.\end{split}

The multiplier
q^{1}_{++-}:=\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{R}}\cdot q_{++-}
is therefore smooth in the region
\mathcal{R}
, and the desired bounds (
7.59
) follow for the symbol
q^{1}_{++-}
.

It remains to prove similar bounds for the multiplier

(7.65)

q^{2}_{++-}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3}):=(\mathbf{1}_{{}^{c}\mathcal{R}}\cdot q_{++-})(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3})=\frac{ip_{3,++-}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3})\mathbf{1}_{{}^{c}\mathcal{R}}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3})\Upsilon(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3})}{|\xi|^{1/2}-|\xi_{1}|^{1/2}-|\xi_{2}|^{1/2}+|\xi_{3}|^{1/2}}.

The point is that the denominator does not vanish in the support of the function
\Upsilon
. Indeed, we notice that if
a,b,c,d\in\mathbb{R}
,
a+b+c+d=0
, and
|d|\leq\min(|a|,|b|,|c|)/20
then

(7.66)

\begin{split}|a|^{1/2}+|b|^{1/2}-|c|^{1/2}-|d|^{1/2}&=\frac{|a|+|b|+2\sqrt{|a||b|}-|c|-|d|-2\sqrt{|c||d|}}{|a|^{1/2}+|b|^{1/2}+|c|^{1/2}+|d|^{1/2}}\\
&\gtrsim\begin{cases}\min(|a|^{1/2},|b|^{1/2})\quad&\text{ in all cases},\\
\max(|a|^{1/2},|b|^{1/2})\quad&\text{ if }|c|\lesssim\min(|a|,|b|).\end{cases}\end{split}

Since
\varphi
is supported in
[-2,2]
, the definition of the function
\Upsilon
in (
7.57
) shows that

q^{2}_{++-}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3})\varphi_{k_{1}}(\xi_{1})\varphi_{k_{2}}(\xi_{2})\varphi_{k_{3}}(\xi_{3})\varphi_{k_{0}}(\xi_{1}+\xi_{2}+\xi_{3})\equiv 0

if
\widetilde{k}_{0}\geq\widetilde{k}_{1}-10
, where
\widetilde{k}_{0}\leq\widetilde{k}_{1}\leq\widetilde{k}_{2}\leq\widetilde{k}_{3}
denotes the nondecreasing rearangement of
k_{0},k_{1},k_{2},k_{3}
. On the other hand, if
\widetilde{k}_{0}\leq\widetilde{k}_{1}-10
then it is easy to see that

\Big\|\frac{\varphi_{k_{0}}(\xi_{1}+\xi_{2}+\xi_{3})\varphi_{k_{1}}(\xi_{1})\varphi_{k_{2}}(\xi_{2})\varphi_{k_{3}}(\xi_{3})}{|\xi_{1}+\xi_{2}+\xi_{3}|^{1/2}-|\xi_{1}|^{1/2}-|\xi_{2}|^{1/2}+|\xi_{3}|^{1/2}}\Big\|_{S^{\infty}}\lesssim 2^{-\widetilde{k}_{1}/2},

due to (
7.66
) and (
7.7
). Moreover, using (
7.55
),

\big\|\varphi_{k_{0}}(\xi_{1}+\xi_{2}+\xi_{3})\varphi_{k_{1}}(\xi_{1})\varphi_{k_{2}}(\xi_{2})\varphi_{k_{3}}(\xi_{3})[p_{3,++-}\mathbf{1}_{{}^{c}\mathcal{R}}\Upsilon](\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3})\big\|_{S^{\infty}}\lesssim 2^{k_{0}/2}2^{\widetilde{k}_{1}/2}2^{3\widetilde{k}_{3}/2}.

Therefore, using (
7.6
),

\big\|\varphi_{k_{0}}(\xi_{1}+\xi_{2}+\xi_{3})\varphi_{k_{1}}(\xi_{1})\varphi_{k_{2}}(\xi_{2})\varphi_{k_{3}}(\xi_{3})q^{2}_{++-}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3})\big\|_{S^{\infty}}\lesssim 2^{k_{0}/2}2^{3\widetilde{k}_{3}/2},

which completes the proof of the bounds (
7.59
) in the case
(\iota_{1}\iota_{2}\iota_{3})=(++-)
.
∎

7.4.
Proof of Proposition
2.1

We start by proving canonical expansions for the nonlinearities
\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{1}_{n}(h,\phi)
,
\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{2}_{n}(h,\phi)
,
\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{1}_{>A}
,
\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{2}_{>A}(h,\phi)
defined in (
7.37
)–(
7.38
), in terms of the original variable
u
.

Lemma 7.5
.

With
u=h+i|\partial_{x}|^{1/2}\phi
as before, for any
n\in[3,A]
we can write

(7.67)

\begin{split}\mathcal{F}&\big\{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{1}_{n}(h,\phi)+i|\partial_{x}|^{1/2}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{2}_{n}(h,\phi)\big\}(\xi)\\
&=\frac{1}{(2\pi R)^{n-1}}\sum_{\iota_{j}\in\{\pm\}}\sum_{\eta_{1},\ldots,\eta_{n}\in\mathbb{Z}/R,\,\eta_{1}+\ldots+\eta_{n}=\xi}b_{\iota_{1}\ldots\iota_{n}}(\eta_{1},\ldots,\eta_{n})\prod_{j=1}^{n}\widehat{u^{\iota_{j}}}(\eta_{j}),\end{split}

where
u^{+}=u
,
u^{-}=\overline{u}
, and the symbols
b_{\iota_{1}\ldots\iota_{n}}
are homogeneous of degree
n-1/2
and satisfy the
S^{\infty}
bounds

(7.68)

\big\|b_{\iota_{1}\ldots\iota_{n}}(\eta_{1},\ldots,\eta_{n})\varphi_{k}(\eta_{1}+\ldots+\eta_{n})\varphi_{k_{1}}(\eta_{1})\cdot\ldots\cdot\varphi_{k_{n}}(\eta_{n})\big\|_{S^{\infty}}\lesssim 2^{k/2}2^{(n-1)\max(k_{1},\ldots,k_{n})},

for any
k,k_{1},\ldots k_{n}\in\overline{\mathbb{Z}}
. Moreover

(7.69)

\big\|\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{1}_{>A}(h,\phi)+i|\partial_{x}|^{1/2}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{2}_{>A}(h,\phi)\big\|_{H^{N_{2}-A-1}}\lesssim\epsilon_{\infty}^{A}B_{2}.

Proof.

The bounds follow easily from the formulas (
7.37
)–(
7.38
) and the estimates on the functions
G_{n},G_{>A},H_{n},H_{>A}
in Lemma
7.2
. At this stage one can actually prove stronger bounds in terms of derivative loss (for example one could replace the Sobolev space
H^{N_{2}-A-1}
with
H^{N_{2}-3}
in (
7.69
)), but such an improvement would not change the following steps.
∎

To prove the main bounds in Proposition
2.1
we need now to understand compositions of operators defined by suitable
S^{\infty}
symbols. We start by defining a suitable class of operators:

Definition 7.6
.

For any
n\in\{1,2,\ldots\}
and
n^{\prime}\in[0,\infty)
we define
\mathcal{O}_{n,n^{\prime}}
as the normed space of
n
-linear operators
T:(H^{n^{\prime}+2}({\mathbb{T}_{R}}))^{n}\to L^{2}(\mathbb{T}_{R})
of the form

(7.70)

\mathcal{F}\{T(f_{1},\ldots,f_{n})\}(\xi)=\frac{1}{(2\pi R)^{n-1}}\sum_{\eta_{1},\ldots,\eta_{n}\in\mathbb{Z}/R,\,\eta_{1}+\ldots+\eta_{n}=\xi}a(\eta_{1},\ldots,\eta_{n})\widehat{f_{1}}(\eta_{1})\cdot\ldots\cdot\widehat{f_{n}}(\eta_{n}),

where the symbol
a
satisfies the
S^{\infty}
bounds

(7.71)

\big\|a(\eta_{1},\ldots,\eta_{n})\varphi_{k}(\eta_{1}+\ldots+\eta_{n})\varphi_{k_{1}}(\eta_{1})\cdot\ldots\cdot\varphi_{k_{n}}(\eta_{n})\big\|_{S^{\infty}}\leq B2^{k/2}2^{(n^{\prime}-1/2)\max(k_{1},\ldots,k_{n})},

for any
k,k_{1},\ldots,k_{n}\in\overline{\mathbb{Z}}
. Here
\|T\|_{\mathcal{O}_{n,n^{\prime}}}:=B
is the smallest constant such that the inequality (
7.71
) holds for any
k,k_{1},\ldots,k_{n}\in\overline{\mathbb{Z}}
.

The point of this definition is that we can prove the following lemma concerning compositions and mapping properties of
\mathcal{O}_{n,n^{\prime}}
operators:

Lemma 7.7
.

(i) Assume
T\in\mathcal{O}_{n,n^{\prime}}
and
S_{j}\in\mathcal{O}_{m_{j},m^{\prime}_{j}}
,
j\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
, are multi-linear operators. We define the multi-linear operator
X
(of order
d:=m_{1}+\ldots+m_{n}
) by

(7.72)

X(f_{11},\ldots f_{1m_{1}},\ldots f_{n1},\ldots,f_{n\,m_{n}}):=T(S_{1}(f_{11},\ldots f_{1m_{1}}),\ldots,S_{n}(f_{n1},\ldots,f_{n\,m_{n}})).

Then
X\in\mathcal{O}_{d,d^{\prime}}
, where
d^{\prime}:=n^{\prime}+m^{\prime}_{1}+\ldots+m^{\prime}_{n}
, and

(7.73)

\|X\|_{\mathcal{O}_{d,d^{\prime}}}\lesssim\|T\|_{\mathcal{O}_{n,n^{\prime}}}\|S_{1}\|_{\mathcal{O}_{m_{1},m^{\prime}_{1}}}\cdot\ldots\cdot\|S_{n}\|_{\mathcal{O}_{m_{n},m^{\prime}_{n}}}.

(ii) Assume
T\in\mathcal{O}_{n,n^{\prime}}
and
f_{1},\ldots,f_{n}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{T}_{R})
satisfy the bounds

(7.74)

\|f_{i}\|_{H^{s_{2}}}\leq A_{2},\qquad\|f_{i}\|_{\dot{W}^{s_{\infty},0}}\leq A_{\infty},

for any
i\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
, where
s_{2}\geq n^{\prime}
and
s_{\infty}\geq 0
. Then

(7.75)

\|T(f_{1},\ldots,f_{n})\|_{H^{s_{2}-n^{\prime}}}\lesssim\|T\|_{\mathcal{O}_{n,n^{\prime}}}A_{2}A_{\infty}^{n-1}.

Moreover, if
s_{\infty}\geq n^{\prime}
then we also have

(7.76)

\|T(f_{1},\ldots,f_{n})\|_{\dot{W}^{s_{\infty}-n^{\prime},0}}\lesssim\|T\|_{\mathcal{O}_{n,n^{\prime}}}A_{\infty}^{n}.

Proof.

(i) Let
a,b_{1},\ldots,b_{n}
denote the symbols associated to the operators
T,S_{1},\ldots,S_{n}
respectively. Then
X
can be written in the form

(7.77)

\begin{split}&\mathcal{F}\{X(f_{11},\ldots,f_{n\,m_{n}})\}(\xi)=\frac{1}{(2\pi R)^{d-1}}\sum_{\eta_{11},\ldots,\eta_{n\,m_{n}}\in\mathbb{Z}/R,\,\eta_{11}+\ldots+\eta_{n\,m_{n}}=\xi}p(\eta_{11},\ldots,\eta_{n\,m_{n}})\\
&\qquad\qquad\times\widehat{f_{11}}(\eta_{11})\cdot\ldots\cdot\widehat{f_{n\,m_{n}}}(\eta_{n\,m_{n}}),\\
&p(\eta_{11},\ldots,\eta_{n\,m_{n}}):=a(\eta_{11}+\ldots+\eta_{1\,m_{1}},\ldots,\eta_{n1}+\ldots+\eta_{n\,m_{n}})\prod_{i=1}^{n}b_{i}(\eta_{i1},\ldots,\eta_{i\,m_{i}}).\end{split}

By multi-linearity we may assume that
\|T\|_{\mathcal{O}_{d,d^{\prime}}}=\|S_{1}\|_{\mathcal{O}_{m_{1},m^{\prime}_{1}}}=\ldots=\|S_{n}\|_{\mathcal{O}_{m_{n},m^{\prime}_{n}}}=1
. We have to prove that for any
k,l_{11},\ldots,l_{n\,m_{n}}\in\overline{\mathbb{Z}}
we have

(7.78)

\big\|p(\eta_{11},\ldots,\eta_{n\,m_{n}})\varphi_{k}(\eta_{11}+\ldots+\eta_{n\,m_{n}})\varphi_{l_{11}}(\eta_{11})\cdot\ldots\cdot\varphi_{l_{n\,m_{n}}}(\eta_{n\,m_{n}})\big\|_{S^{\infty}}\lesssim 2^{k/2}2^{(m^{\prime}-1/2)L},

where
L:=\max(l_{11},\ldots,l_{n\,m_{n}})
.

For any
k,k_{1},\ldots,k_{n}\in\overline{\mathbb{Z}}
let
K_{k;k_{1}\ldots k_{n}}
denote the inverse Fourier transform of the symbol
(\xi_{1},\ldots,\xi_{n})\to a(\xi_{1},\ldots,\xi_{n})\varphi_{k_{1}}(\xi_{1})\cdot\ldots\cdot\varphi_{k_{n}}(\xi_{n})\varphi_{k}(\xi_{1}+\ldots+\xi_{n})
, thus

\begin{split}&a(\xi_{1},\ldots,\xi_{n})\varphi_{k_{1}}(\xi_{1})\cdot\ldots\cdot\varphi_{k_{n}}(\xi_{n})\varphi_{k}(\xi_{1}+\ldots+\xi_{n})\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad=\int_{\mathbb{T}_{R}^{n}}K_{k;k_{1}\ldots k_{n}}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})e^{-i(x_{1}\xi_{1}+\ldots+x_{n}\xi_{n})}\,dx_{1}\ldots dx_{n}.\end{split}

Therefore, using (
7.77
), the inverse Fourier transform of the multiplier in the left-hand side of (
7.78
) is equal to

\begin{split}&G_{k;l_{11}\ldots l_{nm_{n}}}(y_{11},\ldots,y_{n\,m_{n}})=\frac{1}{(2\pi R)^{d}}\sum_{k_{1},\ldots,k_{n}\leq L+2d}\sum_{\eta_{11},\ldots,\eta_{n\,m_{n}}\in\mathbb{Z}/R}e^{i(y_{11}\eta_{11}+\ldots+y_{n\,m_{n}}\eta_{n\,m_{n}})}\\
&\times\prod_{i=1}^{n}\big[b_{i}(\eta_{i1},\ldots,\eta_{i\,m_{i}})\varphi_{l_{i1}}(\eta_{i1})\cdot\ldots\cdot\varphi_{l_{i\,m_{i}}}(\eta_{i\,m_{i}})\varphi_{[k_{i}-2,k_{i}+2]}(\eta_{i1}+\ldots+\eta_{i\,m_{i}})\big]\\
&\times\int_{\mathbb{T}_{R}^{n}}K_{k;k_{1}\ldots k_{n}}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})e^{-i\sum_{i=1}^{n}x_{i}(\eta_{i1}+\ldots+\eta_{i\,m_{i}})}\,dx_{1}\ldots dx_{n}.\end{split}

Letting
L^{i}_{k_{i};l_{i1}\ldots l_{i\,m_{i}}}
denote the inverse Fourier transform of the multiplier

(\eta_{1},\ldots,\eta_{m_{i}})\to b_{i}(\eta_{1},\ldots,\eta_{m_{i}})\varphi_{l_{i1}}(\eta_{1})\cdot\ldots\cdot\varphi_{l_{i\,m_{i}}}(\eta_{m_{i}})\varphi_{[k_{i}-2,k_{i}+2]}(\eta_{1}+\ldots+\eta_{m_{i}}),

we therefore have

\begin{split}&G_{k;l_{11}\ldots l_{nm_{n}}}(y_{11},\ldots,y_{n\,m_{n}})=\sum_{k_{1},\ldots,k_{n}\leq L+2d}\int_{\mathbb{T}_{R}^{n}}K_{k;k_{1}\ldots k_{n}}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})\\
&\qquad\qquad\times\prod_{i=1}^{n}L^{i}_{k_{i};l_{i1}\ldots l_{i\,m_{i}}}(y_{i1}-x_{i},\ldots,y_{i\,m_{i}}-x_{i})\,dx_{1}\ldots dx_{n}.\end{split}

In particular

(7.79)

\|G_{k;l_{11}\ldots l_{nm_{n}}}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{T}_{R}^{d})}\lesssim\sum_{k_{1},\ldots,k_{n}\leq L+2d}\|K_{k;k_{1}\ldots k_{n}}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{T}_{R}^{n})}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\big\|L^{i}_{k_{i};l_{i1}\ldots l_{i\,m_{i}}}\big\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{T}_{R}^{m_{i}})}.

The desired bounds (
7.78
) follow since

\|K_{k;k_{1}\ldots k_{n}}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{T}_{R}^{n})}\lesssim 2^{k/2}2^{(n^{\prime}-1/2)\max(k_{1},\ldots,k_{n})},\qquad\big\|L^{i}_{k_{i};l_{i1}\ldots l_{i\,m_{i}}}\big\|_{L^{1}}\lesssim 2^{k_{i}/2}2^{(m^{\prime}_{i}-1/2)L},

due to the assumption
\|T\|_{\mathcal{O}_{n,n^{\prime}}}=\|S_{1}\|_{\mathcal{O}_{m_{1},m^{\prime}_{1}}}=\ldots=\|S_{n}\|_{\mathcal{O}_{m_{n},m^{\prime}_{n}}}=1
.

(ii) To prove (
7.75
) we may assume
\|T\|_{\mathcal{O}_{n,n^{\prime}}}=1
and define the frequency envelope

(7.80)

\rho_{k}:=\sup_{k^{\prime}\in\overline{\mathbb{Z}},\,i\in\{1,\ldots,n\}}2^{-|k-k^{\prime}|/10}2^{s_{2}k^{\prime}_{+}}\|P_{k^{\prime}}f_{i}\|_{L^{2}},\qquad\text{ for any }k\in\overline{\mathbb{Z}},

where
b_{+}:=\max(b,0)
for any
b\in\overline{\mathbb{Z}}
. Then, using (
7.74
)

(7.81)

\sum_{k\in\overline{\mathbb{Z}}}\rho_{k}^{2}\lesssim A_{2}^{2},\qquad\|P_{k^{\prime}}f_{i}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\rho_{k}2^{|k-k^{\prime}|/10}2^{-s_{2}k^{\prime}_{+}}\text{ for any }k,k^{\prime}\in\overline{\mathbb{Z}},\,i\in\{1,\ldots,n\}.

Using now (
7.5
) and the assumption
\|T\|_{\mathcal{O}_{n,n^{\prime}}}=1
, for any
k\in\overline{\mathbb{Z}}
we estimate

\begin{split}\|P_{k}(T(f_{1},\ldots,f_{n}))\|_{H^{s_{2}-n^{\prime}}}&\lesssim 2^{(s_{2}-n^{\prime})k_{+}}\sum_{k_{1},\ldots,k_{n}\in\mathbb{Z}}\|P_{k}(T(P_{k_{1}}f_{1},\ldots,P_{k_{n}}f_{n}))\|_{L^{2}}\\
&\lesssim A_{\infty}^{n-1}2^{(s_{2}-n^{\prime})k_{+}}2^{k/2}\sum_{k^{\prime}\geq k-4n,\,i\in\{1,\ldots,n\}}2^{(n^{\prime}-1/2)k^{\prime}}\|P_{k^{\prime}}f_{i}\|_{L^{2}},\end{split}

by always estimating the highest frequency in
L^{2}
and all the lower frequencies in
L^{\infty}
. Thus

\begin{split}\|P_{k}(T(f_{1},\ldots,f_{n}))\|_{H^{s_{2}-n^{\prime}}}&\lesssim A_{\infty}^{n-1}2^{(s_{2}-n^{\prime})k_{+}}2^{k/2}\sum_{k^{\prime}\geq k-4n}2^{(n^{\prime}-1/2)k^{\prime}}\rho_{k}2^{(k^{\prime}-k)/10}2^{-s_{2}k^{\prime}_{+}}\\
&\lesssim A_{\infty}^{n-1}\rho_{k}\end{split}

The bounds (
7.75
) follow due to the first estimate in (
7.81
).

The bounds (
7.76
) can be proved in a similar way, by defining a suitable frequency envelope analogous to (
7.80
), based on frequency-localized
L^{\infty}
norms.
∎

We can now complete the proof of all the claims in Proposition
2.1
.

Step 1.
The function
w
is defined as in (
7.34
) and (
7.56
),

(7.82)

\begin{split}&w:=u+\mathcal{A}_{2}(u)+\mathcal{A}_{3}(u),\\
&\mathcal{A}_{2}(u):=A_{2,++}(u,u)+A_{2,+-}(u,\overline{u})+A_{2,--}(\overline{u},\overline{u}),\\
&\mathcal{A}_{3}(u):=A_{3,+++}(u,u,u)+A_{3,++-}(u,u,\overline{u})+A_{3,+--}(u,\overline{u},\overline{u})+A_{3,---}(\overline{u},\overline{u},\overline{u}),\end{split}

where the operators
A_{3,\iota_{1}\iota_{2}\iota}
are defined as in Lemma
7.4
, and

(7.83)

\begin{split}&A_{2,++}(f,g):=(1/8)|\partial_{x}|(H_{0}f\cdot H_{0}g)+(1/8)|\partial_{x}|^{1/2}H_{0}[H_{0}f\cdot|\partial_{x}|^{1/2}g+|\partial_{x}|^{1/2}f\cdot H_{0}g\big],\\
&A_{2,+-}(f,g):=(1/4)|\partial_{x}|(H_{0}f\cdot H_{0}g)-(1/4)|\partial_{x}|^{1/2}H_{0}\big[H_{0}f\cdot|\partial_{x}|^{1/2}g-|\partial_{x}|^{1/2}f\cdot H_{0}g\big],\\
&A_{2,--}(f,g):=(1/8)|\partial_{x}|(H_{0}f\cdot H_{0}g)-(1/8)|\partial_{x}|^{1/2}H_{0}[H_{0}f\cdot|\partial_{x}|^{1/2}g+|\partial_{x}|^{1/2}f\cdot H_{0}g\big].\end{split}

These definitions show easily that
A_{2,\iota_{1}\iota_{2}}\in\mathcal{O}_{2,1}
, while the bounds (
7.59
) show that
A_{3,\iota_{1}\iota_{2}\iota_{3}}\in\mathcal{O}_{3,2}
. This completes the proof of the identities and the bounds (
2.7
), (
2.12
), and the identities in the first line of (
2.9
). The bounds
\|w\|_{H^{N_{2}-2}}\lesssim B_{2}
and
\|w\|_{\dot{W}^{N_{\infty}-2,0}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{\infty}
in (
2.10
) follow from (
7.2
) and Lemma
7.7
(ii).

Step 2.
We show now that the nonlinearity
\mathcal{W}_{\geq 4}
defined in (
7.58
) can be written as

(7.84)

\begin{split}&\mathcal{W}_{\geq 4}=\sum_{n=4}^{A}\sum_{\iota_{1},\ldots,\iota_{n}\in\{+,-\}}W_{n,\iota_{1}\ldots\iota_{n}}(u^{\iota_{1}},\ldots,u^{\iota_{n}})+W_{>A},\\
&W_{n,\iota_{1}\ldots\iota_{n}}\in\mathcal{O}_{n,n-1/2},\qquad\|W_{>A}\|_{H^{N_{2}-A-2}}\lesssim\epsilon_{\infty}^{A}B_{2}.\end{split}

Indeed, the terms in
\mathcal{IN}_{\geq 4}
are already expressed in the desired form in Lemma
7.5
. To express the remaining terms, we notice that, as a consequence of Lemma
7.2

(7.85)

\begin{split}\partial_{t}u+i|\partial_{x}|^{1/2}u&=\sum_{n=2}^{A}[G_{n}(h,\phi)+i|\partial_{x}|^{1/2}H_{n}(h,\phi)]+(G_{>A}+i|\partial_{x}|^{1/2}H_{>A})\\
&=\sum_{n=2}^{A}\sum_{\iota_{1},\ldots,\iota_{n}\in\{+,-\}}L_{n,\iota_{1}\ldots\iota_{n}}(u^{\iota_{1}},\ldots,u^{\iota_{n}})+L_{>A},\end{split}

for some operators
L_{n,\iota_{1}\ldots\iota_{n}}\in\mathcal{O}_{n,n-1/2}
defined by homogeneous symbols of degree
n-1/2
and an error term
L_{>A}
satisfying the bounds
\|L_{>A}\|_{\dot{H}^{N_{2}-2,-1/2}}\lesssim\epsilon_{\infty}^{A}B_{2}
and
\|L_{>A}\|_{\dot{W}^{N_{\infty}-2,-1/4}}\lesssim\epsilon_{\infty}^{A+1}
. The desired representation (
7.84
) follows using Lemma
7.7
, since
A_{3,\iota_{1}\iota_{2}\iota_{3}}\in\mathcal{O}_{3,2}
(see (
7.59
)).

Step 3.
Finally, we prove the conclusions on the nonlinear terms
\mathcal{N}_{n}(w)
stated in Proposition (
2.1
). Notice that, as a consequence of the identities (
7.58
) and (
7.84
),

(7.86)

\partial_{t}w+i|\partial_{x}|^{1/2}w=\sum_{n=3}^{A}\sum_{\iota_{1},\ldots,\iota_{n}\in\{+,-\}}W_{n,\iota_{1}\ldots\iota_{n}}(u^{\iota_{1}},\ldots,u^{\iota_{n}})+W_{>A},

for some operators
W_{n,\iota_{1}\ldots\iota_{n}}\in\mathcal{O}_{n,n-1/2}
defined by homogeneous symbols of degree
n-1/2
and an error term
W_{>A}
satisfying the bounds
\|W_{>A}\|_{\dot{H}^{N_{2}-A-2}}\lesssim\epsilon_{\infty}^{A}B_{2}
. We have to show that we can express the nonlinear terms in the right-hand side of (
7.86
) in terms of the variable
w
.

Assume
n\geq 3
,
S\in\mathcal{O}_{n,n-1/2}
is a multi-linear operator, and
\iota_{1},\ldots\iota_{n}\in\{+,-\}
. We use the identity
u=w-\mathcal{A}_{\geq 2}(u)
in (
7.82
), where
\mathcal{A}_{\geq 2}(u):=\mathcal{A}_{2}(u)+\mathcal{A}_{3}(u)
, to write

(7.87)

\begin{split}&S(u^{\iota_{1}},\ldots,u^{\iota_{n}})=S(w^{\iota_{1}},u^{\iota_{2}},\ldots,u^{\iota_{n}})-S([\mathcal{A}_{\geq 2}(u)]^{\iota_{1}},u^{\iota_{2}},\ldots,u^{\iota_{n}})\\
&=S(w^{\iota_{1}},w^{\iota_{2}},\ldots,u^{\iota_{n}})-S(w^{\iota_{1}},[\mathcal{A}_{\geq 2}(u)]^{\iota_{2}},\ldots,u^{\iota_{n}})-S([\mathcal{A}_{\geq 2}(u)]^{\iota_{1}},u^{\iota_{2}},\ldots,u^{\iota_{n}})\\
&=\ldots=S(w^{\iota_{1}},\ldots,w^{\iota_{n}})-\sum_{a=0}^{n-1}S(w^{\iota_{1}},\ldots,w^{\iota_{a}},[\mathcal{A}_{\geq 2}(u)]^{\iota_{a+1}},\ldots,u^{\iota_{n}})\\
&=S(w^{\iota_{1}},\ldots,w^{\iota_{n}})-\sum_{a=0}^{n-1}S(u^{\iota_{1}}+[\mathcal{A}_{\geq 2}(u)]^{\iota_{1}},\ldots,u^{\iota_{a}}+[\mathcal{A}_{\geq 2}(u)]^{\iota_{a}},[\mathcal{A}_{\geq 2}(u)]^{\iota_{a+1}},\ldots,u^{\iota_{n}}).\end{split}

We can therefore replace
S(u^{\iota_{1}},\ldots,u^{\iota_{n}})
with
S(w^{\iota_{1}},\ldots,w^{\iota_{n}})
at the expense of errors of higher homogeneity; more precisely we can write

(7.88)

S(u^{\iota_{1}},\ldots,u^{\iota_{n}})=S(w^{\iota_{1}},\ldots,w^{\iota_{n}})+\sum_{a=n+1}^{\min(3n,A)}\sum_{\zeta_{1},\ldots,\zeta_{a}\in\{+,-\}}S^{\prime}_{a,\zeta_{1}\ldots\zeta_{a}}(u^{\zeta_{1}},\ldots,u^{\zeta_{a}})+S^{\prime}_{>A}

for some multi-linear operators
S^{\prime}_{a,\zeta_{1}\ldots\zeta_{a}}\in\mathcal{O}_{a,a-1/2}
and an error term
S^{\prime}_{>A}
satisfying the Sobolev bounds
\|S^{\prime}_{>A}\|_{H^{N_{2}-A-2}}\lesssim\epsilon_{\infty}^{A}B_{2}
. The error bounds are obtained by applying Lemma
7.7
(ii) to the terms of homogeneity
\geq A+1
in the right-hand side of (
7.87
), and using the bounds
\|\mathcal{A}_{l}(u)\|_{H^{N_{2}-l}}\lesssim\epsilon_{\infty}^{l}B_{2}
and
\|\mathcal{A}_{l}(u)\|_{\dot{W}^{N_{\infty}-l,0}}\lesssim\epsilon_{\infty}^{l+1}
,
l\in\{2,3\}
.

We can now apply the formula (
7.88
) recursively to the terms in the right-hand side of (
7.86
) to conclude that

(7.89)

\partial_{t}w+i|\partial_{x}|^{1/2}w=\sum_{n=3}^{A}\sum_{\iota_{1},\ldots,\iota_{n}\in\{+,-\}}N_{n,\iota_{1}\ldots\iota_{n}}(u^{\iota_{1}},\ldots,u^{\iota_{n}})+N_{>A},

for some operators
N_{n,\iota_{1}\ldots\iota_{n}}\in\mathcal{O}_{n,n-1/2}
defined by homogeneous symbols of degree
n-1/2
and an error term
N_{>A}
satisfying the bounds
\|W_{>A}\|_{\dot{H}^{N_{2}-A-2}}\lesssim\epsilon_{\infty}^{A}B_{2}
. This suffices to prove all the claims concerning the nonlinearities
\mathcal{N}_{n}(w)
and
\mathcal{N}_{>A}
in Proposition
2.1
, with the exception of the special claims concerning the cubic nonlinearity
\mathcal{N}_{3}
.

Step 4.
We start from the identity (
7.58
). The argument above shows that the cubic nonlinearity
\mathcal{N}_{3}(w)
is defined by

(7.90)

\begin{split}&\widehat{\mathcal{N}_{3}(w)}(\xi)=\frac{1}{(2\pi R)^{2}}\sum_{\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3}\in\mathbb{Z}/R,\,\xi_{1}+\xi_{2}+\xi_{3}=\xi}n_{3,++-}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3})\widehat{w}(\xi_{1})\widehat{w}(\xi_{2})\widehat{\overline{w}}(\xi_{3}),\\
&n_{3,++-}:=p_{3,++-}\mathbf{1}_{{}^{c}\mathcal{R}}(1-\Upsilon).\end{split}

Clearly the cubic symbols
n_{3,\iota_{1}\iota_{2}\iota_{3}}
vanish unless
(\iota_{1}\iota_{2}\iota_{3})=(++-)
, and
n_{3,++-}
is purely imaginary due to the formulas (
7.45
) and (
7.47
)–(
7.54
).

To prove the
C^{1/2}
bounds (
2.13
) we examine the formulas (
7.63
) and the formulas in the second line of (
7.62
). Recalling also the definitions of the domain
\mathcal{R}
and the function
\Upsilon
in (
7.57
), it suffices to prove that if
b:\mathbb{R}^{3}\to\mathbb{R}
is the function defined by

(7.91)

\begin{split}b(\eta,\rho,&\sigma):=\mathbf{1}_{+}(\eta)\mathbf{1}_{+}(\rho)\mathbf{1}_{-}(\sigma)\mathbf{1}_{+}(\eta+\rho+\sigma)(1-\Upsilon)(\eta,\rho,\sigma)\\
&\times\big\{|\eta+\rho+\sigma||\sigma|^{1/2}\big[|\sigma+\eta|+|\sigma+\rho|-|\sigma|\big]-3|\eta+\rho+\sigma|^{3/2}|\eta|^{1/2}|\rho|^{1/2}\big\},\end{split}

where
\mathbf{1}_{+}
and
\mathbf{1}_{-}
denote the characteristic functions of the intervals
[0,\infty)
and
(-\infty,0]
, then

(7.92)

|b(x)-b(y)|\lesssim|x-y|^{1/2}(|x|^{2}+|y|^{2})\qquad\text{ for any }x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{3}.

The definition of the function
\Upsilon
shows that
1-\Upsilon
vanishes if the smallest of the variables
|\eta|,|\rho|,|\sigma|,|\eta+\rho+\sigma|
is sufficiently smaller than the second smallest of these variables. In other words, the smallest and the second smallest of these variables are proportional in the support of
1-\Upsilon
. The definition (
7.91
) then shows that

(7.93)

|b(\eta,\rho,\sigma)|\lesssim\mathbf{1}_{+}(\eta)\mathbf{1}_{+}(\rho)\mathbf{1}_{-}(\sigma)\mathbf{1}_{+}(\eta+\rho+\sigma)(|\eta|^{2}+|\rho|^{2}+|\sigma|^{2})\min(|\eta|,|\rho|,|\sigma|,|\eta+\rho+\sigma|)^{1/2}

for any
\eta,\rho,\sigma\in\mathbb{R}
. In particular, the function
b
is continuous on
\mathbb{R}^{3}
.

To prove the
C^{1/2}
bounds (
7.92
) we may assume by homogeneity that
|x|=1
and
|y|\leq 2
. Letting
y=x+\delta e
it suffices to prove that

(7.94)

|b(x+\delta e)-b(x)|\lesssim\delta^{1/2}\qquad\text{ for any }x,e\in\mathbb{S}^{2}\text{ and }\delta\in[0,4].

In proving (
7.94
) we may assume that
\delta
is small,
\delta\leq 1/10
. The bounds follow from (
7.93
) if
x=(\eta,\rho,\sigma)\in\mathbb{S}^{2}
has the property that
\min(|\eta|,|\rho|,|\sigma|,|\eta+\rho+\sigma|)\leq 2\delta
. On the other hand, if
\eta,\rho,\eta+\rho+\sigma\geq 2\delta
,
\eta\leq-2\delta
, and
|x|=|\eta,\rho,\sigma|=1
then the definition (
7.91
) shows that the function
b
satisfies the stronger Lipschitz bounds

|b(x+\delta e)-b(x)|\lesssim\delta\min(|\eta|,|\rho|,|\sigma|,|\eta+\rho+\sigma|)^{-1/2}.

This suffices to prove (
7.94
) in all cases, which completes the proof of Proposition
2.1
.

8.
A set of divergent diagrams

In this section we present a set of couples that produce divergent contributions,

in the case when
T_{1}\sim\epsilon^{-(8/3)-}
.
This demonstrates
the optimality of our choice
T_{1}\sim\epsilon^{-(8/3)+}
in Theorem
1.1
,
(at least for our current arguments).
In this section, we assume
T_{1}=\epsilon^{-\alpha^{\prime}}
with some fixed
\alpha^{\prime}>(8/3)
.

Scenario
. Consider the following scenario in a tree where
each branching node is an I-branching node and has exactly 3 children nodes. Assume
\mathfrak{n}_{j+1}
is a child node of
\mathfrak{n}_{j}
(where
0\leq j\leq 4q
), and the other children nodes
(\mathfrak{m}_{j},\mathfrak{p}_{j})
of
\mathfrak{n}_{j}
are all leaves. Assume each
\mathfrak{n}_{j}
has sign
+
and
(\mathfrak{m}_{j},\mathfrak{p}_{j})
has sign
(-,+)
. Moreover, assuming they have the following pairing structure:

•

\mathfrak{m}_{2j}
is paired with
\mathfrak{p}_{2j+2}
for each
0\leq j\leq 2q-1
;

•

(\mathfrak{m}_{2j-1},\mathfrak{p}_{2j-1})
is paired with
(\mathfrak{p}_{2\pi(j)-1},\mathfrak{m}_{2\pi(j)-1})

for an involution
\pi
from
\{1,\cdots,2q\}
to itself.

An example with
q=2
is shown in Figure
4
, where
\pi=(3,4,1,2)
.

Figure 4.
An example of a problematic scenario with
q=2
and
\pi=(3,4,1,2)
. Here the leaves of the same color are paired (with the bracket after the leaf labels representing the number of its pair); for illustration, we also draw separate pictures that include the
\mathfrak{m}_{j}
and
\mathfrak{p}_{j}
for even and odd
j
separately.

Regarding the above example, we (heuristically) demonstrate the following points:

(1)

For an individual structure
\mathcal{R}
as above that is contained in a couple
\mathcal{Q}
,
if its size
q
is large enough, then the corresponding sub-expression in
\mathcal{K}_{\mathcal{Q}}

involving this structure
\mathcal{R}
diverges in the limit
\epsilon\to 0
;

(2)

This divergence is a
pure 1D phenomenon
;

(3)

There does not seem to be the same cancellation between different choices
of
\mathcal{R}
, similar to the one involving the irregular chains in Section
4
.

About (1)
:
Consider a decoration of
\mathcal{R}
where
k_{\mathfrak{n}_{j}}=k_{j}
and
(k_{\mathfrak{m}_{j}},k_{\mathfrak{p}_{j}})=(\ell_{j},p_{j})
;
for simplicity we will ignore the
\varphi_{\leq K_{\mathrm{tr}}}
cutoffs, the phase corrections
\Gamma_{0}
and
\Gamma_{1}
,
and the symbol
n_{3}
from the equation.
Now, from the definition (
3.36
) for
\mathcal{K}_{\mathcal{Q}}
,
by arguments similar to the irregular chain in Section
4
, we can extract its part that involves
\mathcal{R}
as

(8.1)

\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}:=\sum_{(k_{j},\ell_{j},p_{j})}^{(*)}\prod_{j=0}^{4q-1}\psi(\ell_{j})\psi(p_{j+1})\cdot\int_{t^{\prime}>t_{1}>\cdots>t_{4q}>t^{\prime\prime}}\prod_{j=0}^{4q}e^{iT_{1}\Omega_{j}t_{j}}\,\mathrm{d}t_{1}\cdots\mathrm{d}t_{4q}.

Here in (
8.1
):

•

The summation is taken over all
(k_{j}:1\leq j\leq 4q)
,
(m_{j}:0\leq j\leq 4q-1)
and
(p_{j}:1\leq j\leq 4q)
, with the values of
(k_{0},p_{0},k_{4q+1},\ell_{4q})=(k_{\mathfrak{n}_{0}},k_{\mathfrak{p}_{0}},k_{\mathfrak{n}_{4q+1}},k_{\mathfrak{m}_{4q}})
being fixed, and under the pairing assumptions
\ell_{2j}=p_{2j+2}\,(0\leq j\leq 2q-1)
and
\ell_{2j-1}=p_{2\pi(j)-1}\,(1\leq j\leq 2q)
.

•

In the integral in (
8.1
), we have
t_{j}=t_{\mathfrak{n}_{j}}\,(1\leq j\leq 4q)
and
(t^{\prime},t^{\prime\prime})=(t_{\mathfrak{n}_{0}},t_{\mathfrak{n}_{4q+1}})
, and

(8.2)

\Omega_{j}:=|k_{j}|^{1/2}-|k_{j+1}|^{1/2}+|\ell_{j}|^{1/2}-|p_{j}|^{1/2}.

To estimate
\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}
, we restrict the decoration to

\displaystyle|\ell_{j}-p_{j}|=|k_{j}-k_{j+1}|\leq T_{1}^{-1}\qquad\mbox{for each \emph{odd} $j$,}

and
|k_{0}-p_{0}|\leq T_{1}^{-1}
. Note that by pairing,

\displaystyle|k_{2}-p_{2}|=|k_{2}-m_{0}|\leq|k_{2}-k_{1}|+|k_{1}-m_{0}|=|\ell_{1}-p_{1}|+|k_{0}-p_{0}|\lesssim T_{1}^{-1},

and, similarly,

\displaystyle|k_{j}-p_{j}|=|\ell_{j}-k_{j+1}|\lesssim T_{1}^{-1}\qquad\mbox{for each \emph{even} $j$}

by induction.
By (
8.2
), for
each

j
(even or odd),
we always have
|\Omega_{j}|\lesssim T_{1}^{-1}
, so there is no oscillation in the time integral in (
8.1
).
Therefore, ignoring the constants that depend only on
q
,
we see the (absolute value of the) time integral in (
8.1
) is bounded below by
O(1)
.

It then suffices to estimate the number of choices for the decoration
(k_{j},\ell_{j},p_{j})
. For this, we first fix the values of
(\ell_{j},p_{j})
for each
odd

j
; by pairing and the assumption
|\ell_{j}-p_{j}|\leq T_{1}^{-1}
, we get
(R^{2}T_{1}^{-1})^{q}
choices. Once they are fixed, we then only need to fix the values of
k_{j}
for even
j
(as they uniquely determine the values of
k_{j}
for odd
j
and thus all the remaining decorations), which has
R^{2q}
choices. This leads to the estimate

(8.3)

|\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}|\gtrsim(R^{4}T_{1}^{-1})^{q}.

Finally, if we take into account the pre-factors in (
3.36
) (that involve the structure
\mathcal{R}
), which is

(8.4)

(\epsilon R^{-1/2})^{8q}T_{1}^{4q}

(because the number
r(\mathcal{R})
of leaves in
\mathcal{R}
equals
8q
,
while the number
n_{I}(\mathcal{R})
of I-branching nodes in
\mathcal{R}
equals
4q
),
we get

(8.5)

(R^{4}T_{1}^{-1})^{q}\cdot(\epsilon R^{-1/2})^{8q}T_{1}^{4q}=(T_{1}^{3}\epsilon^{8})^{q}=\epsilon^{(8-3\alpha^{\prime})q}

which diverges as
\epsilon\to 0
, assuming
\alpha^{\prime}>8/3
.

About (2):
Suppose we work in dimension
d\geq 2
instead of
d=1
, then the bound (
8.3
) should be replaced by

(8.6)

|\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}|\gtrsim(R^{4d}T_{1}^{-d})^{q},

and the pre-factor in (
8.4
) should be replaced by

(8.7)

(\epsilon R^{-d/2})^{8q}T_{1}^{4q},

hence instead of (
8.5
) we have

(8.8)

(R^{4d}T_{1}^{-d})^{q}\cdot(\epsilon R^{-d/2})^{8q}T_{1}^{4q}=(T_{1}^{4-d}\epsilon^{8})^{q},

which is bounded as long as
d\geq 2
and
T_{1}\leq T_{\mathrm{kin}}:=\epsilon^{-4}

(which allows us to cover the conjectured kinetic time).

About (3):
A key feature of the cancellation in Section
4
is the following:
suppose two irregular chains
\mathcal{H}
and
\mathcal{H}^{\prime}
are twists of each other,
then there exists a bijection between decorations of
\mathcal{H}
and decorations of
\mathcal{H}^{\prime}

that preserves the
exact expressions of
\Omega_{\mathfrak{n}}
for each branching node
\mathfrak{n}
.
This relies on the fact that the molecules for
\mathcal{H}
and
\mathcal{H}^{\prime}
are

exactly the same up to directions of all the bonds
.
However, by examining the structure of the molecules corresponding to
\mathcal{R}
,
one can see that there does not exist such a correspondence between different choices of
\mathcal{R}
.
Therefore it is unlikely the terms for different
\mathcal{R}
can exhibit cancellations
similar to those already observed in the literature.

For example, consider the (partial) molecule
\mathbb{M}
corresponding to the structure in
Figure
4
, which has the shape as in Figure
5
.

Figure 5.
The molecule
\mathbb{M}
corresponding to the structure
\mathcal{R}
in Figure
4
.
Here each atom
v_{j}
corresponds to the I-branching node
\mathfrak{n}_{j}
,
with each edge marked by the corresponding vector in the decoration.
Each of the two double bonds is understood as consisting of two bonds of opposite direction.

Now, suppose another structure
\mathcal{R}^{\prime}
corresponds to the same molecule
\mathbb{M}
in Figure
5
,
with the same directions of bonds etc.
Assume
\mathcal{R}^{\prime}
has the same structure as
\mathcal{R}
in Figure
4
,
where the atoms
v_{j}
in
\mathbb{M}
correspond to I-branching nodes
\mathfrak{n}_{j}
in
\mathcal{R}
and
\mathfrak{n}_{j}^{\prime}
in
\mathcal{R}^{\prime}
,
where (say)
\mathfrak{n}_{0}
and
\mathfrak{n}_{0}^{\prime}
both have sign
+
.
Then, since the bond between
v_{0}
and
v_{1}
goes from
v_{1}
to
v_{0}
,
by Definition
5.1
, we know that both
\mathfrak{n}_{1}
and
\mathfrak{n}_{1}^{\prime}
must have sign
+
.
In the same way, all nodes
\mathfrak{n}_{j}
and
\mathfrak{n}_{j}^{\prime}

must have sign
+
5
5

5

In contrast, for two congruence irregular chains,
while
\mathfrak{n}_{0}
must have the same sign, the subsequent
\mathfrak{n}_{j}
may not have the same sign.
,
so by symmetry we may assume
\mathfrak{n}_{j}
is the second child node of
\mathfrak{n}_{j-1}
,
and
\mathfrak{n}_{j}^{\prime}
is the second child of
\mathfrak{n}_{j-1}^{\prime}
.
By considering the other bonds in
\mathbb{M}
and proceeding in this way,
we see that
\mathcal{R}
must be equal to
\mathcal{R}^{\prime}
(up to symmetry) if they correspond to the same molecule,
thus they give rise to the same terms that do not cancel.
